The Saeima has adopted and the President has proclaimed the following law:

Criminal Law

GENERAL PART

Chapter I General Provisions

Section 1. Basis for Criminal Liability

(1)	Only a person who is guilty of committing a criminal offence may be held criminally liable and punished, that is, a person who intentionally or through negligence has committed an act provided for in this Law which has all the elements of a criminal offence.

(2)	A person may be found guilty of committing a criminal offence and a criminal penalty may be imposed by a court judgment and in accordance with law.

(3)	In cases provided for by law, a person is also found guilty of committing a criminal offence and a penalty is imposed by a prosecutor by issuing a penal order.

(4)	An act may not be recognised as criminal by applying the law by analogy.

(5)	No one may be tried or punished again for an act for which he or she has already been acquitted or punished by a decision in a criminal case or administrative offence case adopted and entered into force in accordance with the procedure provided for by law. The aforementioned does not exclude re-examination of a case in accordance with law if newly discovered circumstances have been established or if a substantial violation of substantive or procedural law has been committed in the previous proceedings which could have affected the outcome of the case.
(With amendments made by the laws of 28.09.2005., 13.12.2012. and 15.05.2014., which enter into force on 14.06.2014.)

2. pants. Territorial Scope of the Criminal Law

(1)	A person who has committed a criminal offence in the territory of Latvia shall be held liable in accordance with this Law.

(2)	If a criminal offence in the territory of Latvia has been committed by a foreign diplomatic representative or another person who, in accordance with laws in force or international agreements binding on the Republic of Latvia, is not subject to the jurisdiction of the Republic of Latvia, the question of bringing this person to criminal liability shall be decided through diplomatic channels or in accordance with mutual agreement between states.

3. pants

Scope of the Criminal Law on Aircraft, Sea and River Vessels Outside the Territory of Latvia

A person who has committed a criminal offence outside the territory of Latvia while on board an aircraft, sea or river vessel or other floating craft, if this craft is registered in the Republic of Latvia and if international agreements binding on the Republic of Latvia do not provide otherwise, shall be brought to liability in accordance with this Law.

4. pants. Scope of the Criminal Law Outside the Territory of Latvia

(1)	Citizens of Latvia, non-citizens or foreigners who have a permanent residence permit in the Republic of Latvia, for an offence committed in the territory of another state or outside the territory of any state, regardless of whether it is recognised as criminal and punishable at the place of commission, shall be brought to liability in the territory of Latvia in accordance with this Law.

(11) For an offence committed in the territory of another state or outside the territory of any state, regardless of whether it is recognised as criminal and punishable at the place of commission, if it has been committed by a natural person in the interests of, for the benefit of, or as a result of inadequate supervision or control of a legal person registered in the Republic of Latvia, coercive measures provided for in this Law may be applied to the legal person.

(2)	Members of the armed forces of the Republic of Latvia who are deployed outside the territory of Latvia shall be brought to liability for criminal offences in accordance with this Law, unless international agreements binding on the Republic of Latvia provide otherwise.

(3)	Foreigners who do not have a permanent residence permit in the Republic of Latvia and who have committed serious or particularly serious crimes in the territory of another state directed against the interests of the Republic of Latvia or its inhabitants, regardless of the laws of the state in whose territory the crime was committed, shall be brought to criminal liability in accordance with this Law if they have not been brought to criminal liability or brought to trial in accordance with the laws of the state where the crime was committed.

(4)	Foreigners who do not have a permanent residence permit in the Republic of Latvia and who have committed a criminal offence in the territory of another state or outside the territory of any state, regardless of the laws of the state where the offence was committed, shall be brought to liability in accordance with this Law in cases provided for in international agreements binding on the Republic of Latvia, if they have not been brought to criminal liability or brought to trial in the territory of another state for this offence.
(Ar grozījumiem, kas izdarīti ar 17.10.2002., 16.12.2004., 21.05.2009., 21.10.2010. un 25.09.2014. likumu, kas
stājas spēkā 29.10.2014.)

5. pants. Temporal Scope of the Criminal Law

(1)	The criminality and punishability of an offence (act or omission) shall be determined by the law that was in force at the time of the commission of that offence.

(2)	A law that recognises an offence as non-punishable, mitigates punishment or is otherwise favourable to a person, unless the respective law provides otherwise, shall have retroactive effect, namely, it shall apply to offences committed before the entry into force of the respective law, as well as to a person who is serving a sentence or has served a sentence but for whom punishability has been preserved.

(3)	A law that recognises an offence as punishable, increases punishment or is otherwise unfavourable to a person shall not have retroactive effect.

(4)	A person who has committed a crime against humanity, a crime against peace, a war crime and has participated in genocide shall be punishable regardless of the time of commission of these crimes.

II	nodaļa Noziedzīgs nodarījums

6. pants. Concept of a Criminal Offence

(1)	A criminal offence shall be recognised as a harmful offence (act or omission) committed with intent (intentionally) or through negligence, which is provided for in this Law and for the commission of which a criminal penalty is prescribed.

(2)	An offence (act or omission) that has the elements of an offence provided for in this Law but has been committed in circumstances that exclude criminal liability shall not be recognised as criminal.
(Ar grozījumiem, kas izdarīti ar 13.12.2012. likumu, kas stājas spēkā 01.04.2013.)

7. pants. Classification of Criminal Offences

(1)	According to the nature and harmfulness of the threat to the interests of a person or society, criminal offences shall be divided into criminal violations and crimes. Crimes shall be divided as follows: less serious crimes, serious crimes and particularly serious crimes.

(2)	A criminal violation is an offence for which this Law provides deprivation of liberty for a period from fifteen days but not exceeding three months (short-term deprivation of liberty), or a lighter type of punishment.

(3)	A less serious crime is an intentional offence for which this Law provides deprivation of liberty for a period exceeding three months but not exceeding three years, as well as an offence committed through negligence for which this Law provides deprivation of liberty for a period not exceeding eight years.

(4)	A serious crime is an intentional offence for which this Law provides deprivation of liberty for a period exceeding three years but not exceeding eight years, as well as an offence committed through negligence for which this Law provides deprivation of liberty for a period exceeding eight years.

(5)	A particularly serious crime is an intentional offence for which this Law provides deprivation of liberty for a period exceeding eight years, or life imprisonment.

(6)	If for a crime this Law provides deprivation of liberty for a period not exceeding five years, then for the respective crime it shall also provide a lighter type of punishment.
(Ar grozījumiem, kas izdarīti ar 21.05.2009., 01.12.2011., 13.12.2012. un 17.12.2020. likumu, kas stājas spēkā 01.01.2022.)

8. pants. Forms of Guilt

(1)	Only a person who has committed it with intent (intentionally) or through negligence shall be recognised as guilty of a criminal offence.

(2)	When determining the form of guilt of a person who has committed a criminal offence, the mental attitude of this person towards the objective elements of the criminal offence must be established.

9. pants. Commission of a Criminal Offence with Intent (Intentionally)

(1)	A criminal offence shall be recognised as committed with intent (intentionally) if the person has committed it with direct or indirect intent.

(2)	A criminal offence shall be recognised as committed with direct intent if the person was aware of the harmfulness of his or her act or omission and consciously performed or permitted it, or was aware of the harmfulness of his or her act or omission, foresaw the harmful consequences of the offence and desired their occurrence.

(3)	A criminal offence shall be recognised as committed with indirect intent if the person was aware of the harmfulness of his or her act or omission, foresaw the harmful consequences of the offence and, although he or she did not desire these consequences, nevertheless consciously permitted their occurrence.
(13.12.2012. likuma redakcijā, kas stājas spēkā 01.04.2013.)

This excerpt sits under: Izdevējs: Saeima Veids: likums Pieņemts: 17.06.1998.

10. pants. Commission of a Criminal Offence through Negligence

(1)	A criminal offence shall be deemed to have been committed through negligence if a person has committed it through criminal overconfidence or criminal carelessness.

(2)	A criminal offence shall be deemed to have been committed through criminal overconfidence if a person has foreseen the possibility of harmful consequences of their action or inaction occurring, but has frivolously relied on being able to prevent them.

(3)	A criminal offence shall be deemed to have been committed through criminal carelessness if a person has not foreseen the possibility of harmful consequences of their action or inaction occurring, although according to the specific circumstances of the offence they should have and could have foreseen the said harmful consequences.

(4)	An offence provided for in this law is not criminally punishable if a person did not foresee, should not have and could not have foreseen the possibility of harmful consequences of their action or inaction occurring.
(13.12.2012. likuma redakcijā, kas stājas spēkā 01.04.2013.)

11. pants. Age at Which Criminal Liability Arises

A natural person who has reached the age of fourteen years by the day of commission of a criminal offence may be held criminally liable. A minor, that is, a person who has not reached the age of fourteen years, may not be held criminally liable.

12. pants. Liability of a Natural Person in a Matter Concerning a Legal Person

For a criminal offence committed by a natural person in the interests of a private law legal person, for the benefit of that person or as a result of inadequate supervision or control by that person, the respective natural person shall be held criminally liable, and coercive measures provided for in this law may be applied to the legal person.
(14.03.2013. likuma redakcijā, kas stājas spēkā 01.04.2013.)

13. pants. Insanity

(1)	A person who at the time of commission of an offence was in a state of insanity, that is, due to mental disorders or mental retardation was unable to understand their actions or control them, may not be held criminally liable.

(2)	The court may apply coercive measures of a medical nature provided for in this law to a person who has been found to be insane.
(Ar grozījumiem, kas izdarīti ar 20.03.2014. likumu, kas stājas spēkā 03.04.2014.)

14. pants. Diminished Capacity

(1)	If at the time of commission of a criminal offence a person, due to mental disorders or mental retardation, was not fully capable of understanding their actions or controlling them, that is, was in a state of diminished capacity, the court may, depending on the specific circumstances of the offence, mitigate the sentence to be imposed or release that person from punishment.

(2)	The court shall apply coercive measures of a medical nature provided for in this law to a person who has been found to have diminished capacity.

15. pants. Completed and Uncompleted Criminal Offence

(1)	A criminal offence shall be deemed completed if it has all the elements of a criminal offence provided for in this law.

(2)	Preparation for a crime and an attempt to commit a crime constitute an uncompleted criminal offence.

(3)	Preparation for a crime shall be deemed to be the search for or adaptation of means or instruments or other intentional creation of favourable circumstances for the commission of an intentional crime, if moreover it has not been continued for reasons beyond the will of the guilty person. Criminal liability arises only for preparation for serious or particularly serious crimes.

(4)	An attempt to commit a crime shall be deemed to be a deliberate action (inaction) directly aimed at its intentional commission, if

the crime has not been completed for reasons beyond the will of the guilty person.

(5)	Liability for preparation for a crime or an attempt to commit a crime arises in accordance with the same article of this law that provides for liability for the specific offence.

(6)	A person may not be held criminally liable for an attempt to commit a misdemeanour.

16. pants. Voluntary Withdrawal

(1)	Voluntary withdrawal from the commission of a criminal offence is the complete cessation of a criminal offence initiated by a person according to that person's will, while being aware that there is a possibility to complete the criminal offence.

(2)	A person who has voluntarily withdrawn from the commission of a criminal offence may not be held criminally liable. Such a person shall be liable only if the offence actually committed by them contains the elements of another criminal offence.

17. pants. Perpetrator of a Criminal Offence

A person who has directly committed a criminal offence themselves or has used another person in its commission who, in accordance with the provisions of this law, may not be held criminally liable, shall be deemed to be the perpetrator of a criminal offence.

18. pants. Participation of Several Persons in a Criminal Offence

Joint deliberate participation of two or more persons in the commission of an intentional criminal offence is co-perpetration or complicity.

19. pants. Co-perpetration

Co-perpetration (joint perpetration) shall be deemed to be a deliberate criminal action by which two or more persons have directly committed an intentional criminal offence jointly and knowingly (that is, a group). Each of these persons is a participant (co-perpetrator) in the criminal offence.

20. pants. Complicity

(1)	Complicity shall be deemed to be a deliberate action or inaction by which a person (accomplice) has participated jointly with another person (perpetrator) in the commission of an intentional criminal offence, but has not themselves been its direct perpetrator. Accomplices to a criminal offence are organisers, instigators, aiders and abettors.

(2)	An organiser shall be deemed to be a person who has organised or directed the commission of a criminal offence.

(3)	An instigator shall be deemed to be a person who has induced another person to commit a criminal offence.

(4)	An aider and abettor shall be deemed to be a person who has knowingly facilitated the commission of a criminal offence by giving advice, instructions, means or removing obstacles to its commission, as well as a person who has promised in advance to conceal the perpetrator or accomplice of a criminal offence, the instruments and means of commission of a criminal offence, the traces of a criminal offence or objects obtained through criminal means, or has promised in advance to acquire or dispose of such objects.

(5)	An accomplice to criminal offences shall be held liable in accordance with the same article of this law that provides for the liability of the perpetrator.

(6)	Individual elements of a criminal offence relating to the perpetrator or accomplice of a criminal offence do not affect the liability of other co-perpetrators or accomplices.

(7)	If an accomplice was not aware of any of the criminal offences committed by the perpetrator and other accomplices, they may not be held criminally liable for them.

(8)	If the perpetrator has not completed the crime for reasons beyond their will, the accomplices are liable for complicity in an attempt to commit the respective crime. If the perpetrator has not commenced the commission of the crime, the accomplices are liable for preparation for the respective crime.

(9)	Voluntary withdrawal of an organiser and instigator from the completion of a criminal offence shall be deemed to be such only in cases where they have done everything possible in a timely manner to prevent the commission of the criminal offence intended with their complicity, and this offence has not been committed. An aider and abettor may not be held criminally liable if they have voluntarily withdrawn the promised assistance before the commencement of the criminal offence.

21. pants. Organised Group

(1)	An organised group is an association formed by more than two persons, which has been created for the purpose of jointly committing one or more crimes and whose members have divided duties in accordance with a prior agreement.

(2)	Liability for commission of a crime in an organised group arises for a person in the cases provided for in this law for the formation and direction of a group, participation in the preparation of a serious or particularly serious crime or commission of a crime regardless of the person's role in the jointly committed offence.
(Ar grozījumiem, kas izdarīti ar 25.04.2002. un 13.12.2012. likumu, kas stājas spēkā 01.04.2013.)

22. Section. Previously Unpromised Concealment and Failure to Report

(1)	Previously unpromised concealment of a perpetrator or accomplice of a crime, as well as of instruments and means of committing a crime or traces of a crime, and failure to report a crime do not constitute complicity, and criminal liability for such conduct arises only in cases provided for in this Law.

(2)	The fiancé(e), spouse, parents, children, brothers and sisters, grandparents and grandchildren of the person who committed the crime, as well as a person with whom the natural person who committed the crime lives together and with whom he or she has a common (undivided) household, shall not be liable for previously unpromised concealment and failure to report.

(3)	In cases provided for by law, other persons shall also not be liable for failure to report.
(With amendments made by the Laws of 25.09.2014 and 06.06.2019, which enter into force on 03.07.2019.)

23. Section. Single (Unified) Criminal Offence

(1)	A single (unified) criminal offence is one act (action or omission) that has the elements of one criminal offence, or two or more interrelated criminal offences covered by a single intent of the guilty person and which correspond to the elements of only one criminal offence.

(2)	A single (unified) criminal offence also comprises continuing and continuous criminal offences.

(3)	A single continuing criminal offence comprises several interrelated identical criminal actions directed towards a common objective, if they are covered by a single intent of the guilty person, and therefore in their totality they constitute one criminal offence.

(4)	A single continuous criminal offence is the continuous realisation of one criminal offence (action or omission) connected with the subsequent continuous non-performance of those duties which the law has imposed on the guilty person under threat of criminal prosecution.

(5)	(Deleted by the Law of 13.12.2012)
(With amendments made by the Law of 13.12.2012, which enters into force on 01.04.2013.)

24. Section. Plurality of Criminal Offences

(1)	Plurality of criminal offences exists when one person commits (or permits) two or more independent acts (action or omission) that correspond to the elements of several criminal offences, or when a person commits (or permits) one act (action or omission) that corresponds to the elements of at least two different criminal offences.

(2)	Plurality of criminal offences comprises aggregate of criminal offences and recidivism.

(3)	Plurality of criminal offences also comprises such criminal offences for which a sentence imposed abroad is being executed in Latvia.
(With amendments made by the Laws of 20.06.2002 and 13.12.2012, which enters into force on 01.04.2013.)

25. Section. Repetition of Criminal Offences

(Deleted by the Law of 13.12.2012, which enters into force on 01.04.2013.)

26. Section. Aggregate of Criminal Offences

(1)	An aggregate of criminal offences comprises one act or several acts committed by one person that correspond to the elements of two or more criminal offences, if this person has not been convicted of any of these criminal offences and the limitation period for criminal liability has not expired either.

(2)	An act committed by a person that corresponds to the elements of several different interrelated criminal offences constitutes an ideal aggregate of criminal offences.

(3)	Two or more unrelated acts committed by a person that correspond to the elements of several criminal offences constitute a real aggregate of criminal offences.

(4)	An aggregate of criminal offences does not comprise an act for the commission of which a person has been released from criminal liability.

(5)	If one criminal offence corresponds to a general and a special provision provided for in the Special Part of this Law, then an aggregate of criminal offences does not arise and criminal liability arises only under the special provision.
(With amendments made by the Law of 13.12.2012, which enters into force on 01.04.2013.)

27. Section. Recidivism of Criminal Offences

Recidivism of a criminal offence comprises a new intentional criminal offence committed by a person after this person's conviction for a previously committed intentional criminal offence, if the criminal record for it has not been removed or has not been expunged in the manner prescribed by law at the time of committing the new criminal offence.
(In the wording of the Law of 12.11.2015, which enters into force on 02.12.2015.)

Chapter III
Circumstances Excluding Criminal Liability

28. Section. Types of Circumstances Excluding Criminal Liability

Circumstances that exclude criminal liability, even though actions committed in these circumstances correspond to the elements of a criminal offence provided for in this Law, are necessary defence, apprehension causing harm to a person, extreme necessity, justifiable professional risk and execution of a criminal order or criminal instruction.

29. Section. Necessary Defence

(1)	Necessary defence is an action committed in defence of state or public interests, one's own or another person's rights, as well as a person against an attack or threat of attack in such a way that harm is caused to the attacker. Criminal liability for this action arises if the limits of necessary defence have been exceeded.

(2)	Exceeding the limits of necessary defence is recognised as an obvious disproportion of the defence to the nature and dangerousness of the attack, as a result of which harm is caused to the attacker that was not necessary to prevent or repel the attack.

(3)	Causing harm to an attacker through negligence while repelling an attack is not criminally punishable.

(4)	A person has the right to necessary defence regardless of the possibility to avoid the attack or to

seek assistance from other persons.

30. Section. Putative Defence

(1)	Putative defence exists when the real attack referred to in Section 29 of this Law does not occur, but the person mistakenly believes that such an attack is occurring.

(2)	In cases where the circumstances of the incident have given grounds to assume that a real attack is occurring, but the person who applied defensive measures was not aware that such assumption was mistaken, and moreover could not and should not have been aware of it, the actions of this person are assessed as necessary defence.

(3)	A person who has exceeded the limits of defence that would be permissible in the circumstances of a corresponding real attack is liable as for exceeding the limits of necessary defence.

(4)	A person who causes harm corresponding to the elements of a criminal offence to the putative attacker without being aware of the putative nature of the attack, although in the specific circumstances he or she should have and could have been aware of it, is liable for the respective act as one committed through negligence.

31. Section. Apprehension Causing Harm to a Person

(1)	Apprehension causing harm to a person is an action directed against a person who is committing or has committed a criminal offence. Criminal liability for this action does not arise if there has been no obvious disproportion of the harm caused to the person to the nature of the act, non-compliance or resistance.

(2)	A person who, in carrying out apprehension, has violated the conditions of apprehension is liable for the violation of these conditions.

(3)	If the actions by which harm is caused to the person to be apprehended were not necessary for his or her apprehension, liability for the harm caused arises on general grounds.

(4)	Causing harm to a person to be apprehended through negligence is not criminally punishable.

32. Section. Extreme Necessity

Extreme necessity is an action that a person commits in order to prevent harm that threatens state or public interests, the rights of this person or another person, as well as these or other persons, if the respective harm could not be prevented by other means in the specific circumstances and if the harm caused is less than the harm prevented. Extreme necessity excludes criminal liability.

33. Section. Justified professional risk

(1) Criminal liability does not arise for harm caused by professional activity that has the characteristics of a criminal offence if this activity was carried out to achieve a socially beneficial purpose that could not be achieved in any other way. The professional risk associated with this activity shall be considered justified if the person who took the risk has done everything to prevent harm to legally protected interests.

(2) Risk shall not be recognised as justified if it is knowingly associated with a threat to the life of several persons or with threats to cause an ecological catastrophe or public disaster.

34. Section. Execution of a criminal order and a criminal instruction

(1) The execution of a criminal order or a criminal instruction is justified for the person who executed it only in cases where this person was not aware of the criminal nature of the order or instruction and it was not obvious. Criminal liability in such cases nevertheless arises when crimes against humanity and peace, war crimes and genocide have been committed.

(2) A person who has not executed a criminal order or instruction shall not be held criminally liable.

IV Chapter Punishment

35. Section. Punishment and its purpose

(1) The punishment provided for in the Criminal Law is a coercive measure which, in the name of the State, is imposed by a court within the framework of this Law on a person who is guilty of committing a criminal offence, or in cases provided for by law is determined by a prosecutor by drawing up a penal order.

(2) The purpose of punishment is:

1) to protect public safety;

2) to restore justice;

3) to punish the guilty person for the criminal offence committed;

4) to resocialise the punished person;

5) to ensure that the convicted person and other persons comply with the laws and refrain from committing criminal offences.

(With amendments made by the laws of 28.09.2005 and 13.12.2012, which enter into force on 01.04.2013.)

36. Section. Types of punishment

(1) A person who has committed a criminal offence may be sentenced to one of the following principal punishments:

1) (deleted by the law of 01.12.2011);

2) deprivation of liberty;

3) (deleted by the law of 13.12.2012);

4) (deleted by the law of 13.12.2012);

41) probation supervision;

5) community service;

6) fine.

(2) In addition to the principal punishment, the following additional punishments may be imposed on the convicted person:

1) confiscation of property;

2) expulsion from the Republic of Latvia;

3) probation supervision;

4) community service;

5) fine;

6) restriction of rights.

(3) For a person who has committed a criminal violation, a less serious crime or a serious crime for which deprivation of liberty for up to five years is provided, a prosecutor, by drawing up a penal order, may impose probation supervision, community service or a fine, as well as additional punishments — community service, a fine or

restriction of rights.

(4) The procedure for serving punishment shall be determined in accordance with law.

(With amendments made by the laws of 12.02.2004, 28.09.2005, 08.12.2005, 21.05.2009, 08.07.2011, 01.12.2011,
13.12.2012, 0.03.2016 and 17.12.2020, which enters into force on 01.01.2022.)

37. Section. Death penalty

(Deleted by the law of 01.12.2011, which enters into force on 01.01.2012.)

38. Section. Deprivation of liberty

(1) Deprivation of liberty is the compulsory detention of a person in imprisonment.

(2) Deprivation of liberty shall be imposed for a period from fifteen days to twenty years.

(21) In cases specifically provided for in this Law for criminal violations and crimes for which this Law provides for deprivation of liberty for a period not exceeding five years, short-term deprivation of liberty may be imposed for a period of up to three months.

(3) In cases specifically provided for in this Law, deprivation of liberty may be imposed for life (life imprisonment).

(4) The period of deprivation of liberty shall be determined in years, months and days.

(With amendments made by the laws of 16.06.2009, 13.12.2012, 25.09.2014 and 12.11.2015, which enters into force on 02.12.2015.)

38. 1 Section. Probation supervision

(1) Probation supervision as a principal punishment or additional punishment is the compulsory involvement of a person in social behaviour correction and social rehabilitation measures appropriate to their age, psychological characteristics and level of development.

(2) Probation supervision as a principal punishment may be applied only in the cases mentioned in the Special Part of this Law for a period from one year to five years.

(3) Probation supervision as an additional punishment may be applied only together with deprivation of liberty in the cases mentioned in the Special Part of this Law for a period from one year to three years. In cases specifically provided for in the Special Part of this Law, probation supervision as an additional punishment may also be applied to a person for a longer period, but not exceeding five years. According to the nature of the criminal offence, the court may also impose probation supervision as an additional punishment in cases where this punishment is not provided for in the sanction of the relevant article of the Special Part of this Law, for a period from one year to three years.

(4) The convicted person or the person for whom probation supervision has been imposed by a prosecutor's penal order shall, during the probation supervision period, fulfil the obligations provided for in the law regulating the execution of criminal punishment and imposed by the sentence execution institution.

(5) The execution of probation supervision as an additional punishment shall commence after the serving of the deprivation of liberty sentence. If a person has been conditionally released before the term from the execution of the deprivation of liberty sentence, the execution of probation supervision as an additional punishment shall commence from the moment when the supervision of the person after conditional early release has ended.

(6) The court, upon receipt of an application from the sentence execution institution, may reduce the term of probation supervision or cancel probation supervision if the person for whom probation supervision has been imposed by a court judgment or a prosecutor's penal order has served half of the imposed sentence term, has exemplarily fulfilled the obligations provided for in the law regulating the execution of criminal punishment and imposed by the sentence execution institution, and has cooperated with the sentence execution institution in resolving identified criminogenic problems and achieving socially supportive goals.

(7) If a person for whom probation supervision has been imposed by a court judgment or a prosecutor's penal order, without justifiable reason, fails to fulfil the obligations provided for in the law regulating the execution of criminal punishment or imposed by the sentence execution institution, the court, upon receipt of an application from the sentence execution institution, shall replace the unserved period of the sentence, counting one day of probation supervision as one day of deprivation of liberty, but not exceeding:

1) three months if probation supervision was imposed for a criminal violation;

2) three years if probation supervision was imposed for a less serious crime;

3) five years if probation supervision was imposed for a serious or particularly serious crime.

(In the wording of the law of 17.12.2020 with amendments made by the law of 07.04.2022, which enters into force on 04.05.2022.)

39. Section. Arrest

(Deleted by the law of 13.12.2012, which enters into force on 01.04.2013.)

40. Section. Community Service

(1)	Community service as a principal or supplementary punishment is the compulsory involvement of a person in work necessary for society that is appropriate to their age, psychological characteristics, physical abilities and level of development, during time free from principal work or studies and without remuneration.

(2)	Community service as a principal punishment shall be imposed for a period from forty to two hundred and eighty hours. Community service as a supplementary punishment for a period from forty to one hundred hours may be imposed on a person who has been conditionally convicted or for whom probation supervision has been imposed as a principal punishment.

(3)	Community service is not applicable to persons who are unable to perform it due to physical or mental disorders.

(4)	Upon the proposal of the sentence execution institution, the court may release from serving the sentence a person who has been convicted to community service or for whom community service has been imposed by a prosecutor's penal order, if the community service has been imposed for a period not less than eighty hours, if at least half of the imposed sentence has actually been served and the person has fulfilled the obligations provided for in the law regulating the execution of criminal sentences, has observed the conditions and procedure for the performance of community service, and has also performed the community service in an exemplary manner.

(5)	If a person who has been convicted to community service, or a person for whom community service has been imposed by a prosecutor's penal order, fails to perform it without justifiable reason, the court, upon receipt of an application from the sentence execution institution, shall replace the unserved sentence with short-term deprivation of liberty, calculating four working hours as one day of short-term deprivation of liberty, but not longer than three months.
(In the wording of the Law of 17.12.2020. with amendments made by the Law of 07.04.2022., which enters into force on 04.05.2022.)

41. Section. Fine

(1)	A fine as a principal or supplementary punishment is a sum of money which the court or prosecutor imposes for payment to the state within 30 days in the amounts specified in this Section.

(2)	A fine as a principal punishment, in accordance with the harmfulness of the criminal offence and the financial situation of the guilty person, shall be imposed:

1)	for a criminal violation — in the amount of one to one hundred minimum monthly wages established in the Republic of Latvia;

2)	for a less serious crime — in the amount of three to one thousand minimum monthly wages established in the Republic of Latvia;

3)	for a serious crime for which this Law provides for deprivation of liberty for a period not exceeding five years — in the amount of ten to two thousand minimum monthly wages established in the Republic of Latvia.

(21)	A fine shall be imposed in the amount of minimum monthly wages established in the Republic of Latvia as it is on the day of rendering the judgment, indicating in the judgment the amount of this fine in the monetary units of the Republic of Latvia.

(22)	In accordance with the harmfulness of the criminal offence and the financial situation of the guilty person, the court may impose a fine as a principal punishment also for the commission of a serious crime for which this Law provides for deprivation of liberty for a period exceeding five years, and for the commission of a particularly serious crime, if the crime has not caused the death of a person, has not caused serious bodily injury to at least one person or less serious bodily injury to several persons, is not connected with violence or the threat of violence, is not connected with the illegal circulation of narcotic and psychotropic substances and has not been committed in an organised group, in the amount of three hundred to ten thousand minimum monthly wages established in the Republic of Latvia.

(3)	A fine as a supplementary punishment, in accordance with the harmfulness of the criminal offence and the financial situation of the guilty person, shall be imposed:

1)	for a criminal violation — in the amount of one to one hundred minimum monthly wages established in the Republic of Latvia;

2)	for a less serious crime — in the amount of three to one thousand minimum monthly wages established in the Republic of Latvia;

3)	for a serious crime for which this Law provides for deprivation of liberty for a period not exceeding five years — in the amount of five to two thousand minimum monthly wages established in the Republic of Latvia;

4)	for a serious crime for which this Law provides for deprivation of liberty for a period exceeding five years, and for a particularly serious crime — in the amount of ten to five thousand minimum monthly wages established in the Republic of Latvia.

(4)	The financial situation of the guilty person shall be determined by assessing their ability to pay the fine immediately or to obtain foreseeable income that would allow them to pay the imposed fine within the time period established by law.

(5)	The payment of the fine may be divided into instalments or postponed by the court or prosecutor respectively for a period not exceeding one year from the day when the judgment or penal order has entered into legal force. If the fine exceeds three hundred minimum monthly wages, its payment may be divided into instalments or postponed by the court or prosecutor respectively for a period not exceeding three years from the day when the judgment or penal order has entered into legal force.

(6)	A fine not paid within the established time, if it does not exceed thirty minimum monthly wages, shall be replaced with short-term deprivation of liberty, calculating one minimum monthly wage as four days of short-term deprivation of liberty, but not more than three months; a fine not paid, if it does not exceed two hundred minimum monthly wages, shall be replaced with deprivation of liberty, calculating one minimum monthly wage as four days of deprivation of liberty, but not more than one year; a fine not paid, if it exceeds two hundred minimum monthly wages, shall be replaced with deprivation of liberty, calculating one minimum monthly wage as five days of deprivation of liberty, but not more than five years.

(7)	If the fine or part thereof is paid at a time when the convicted person is serving a sentence of deprivation of liberty in place of the fine, they shall be released or the term of deprivation of liberty shall be reduced in accordance with the part of the fine paid. When reducing the term of the sentence in the manner indicated, the term of deprivation of liberty shall be counted in accordance with the proportion established by the court.
(In the wording of the Law of 16.06.2009. with amendments made by the Laws of 13.12.2012., 12.11.2015., 29.10.2015., 10.03.2016.,
08.06.2017., 06.06.2019., 11.06.2020., 06.07.2021. and 07.04.2022., which enters into force on 04.05.2022.)

42. Section. Confiscation of Property

(1)	Confiscation of property is the compulsory uncompensated alienation to state ownership of property in the ownership of the convicted person. Confiscation of property may be imposed as a supplementary punishment. Property in the ownership of the convicted person which they have transferred to another natural or legal person may also be confiscated.

(2)	Confiscation of property may be imposed only in the cases provided for in the Special Part of this Law.

(3)	The court, when imposing confiscation of property, shall specifically indicate what property is to be confiscated.

(4)	The law establishes the property necessary for the convicted person or persons in their care that is not subject to confiscation.
(In the wording of the Law of 12.02.2004. with amendments made by the Laws of 06.10.2005., 21.05.2009., 13.12.2012. and 22.06.2017., which enters into force on 01.08.2017.)

43. Section. Expulsion from the Republic of Latvia

(1)	A citizen of another state or a person who has a permanent residence permit in another state may be expelled from the Republic of Latvia if the court finds that, taking into account the circumstances of the case and the personality of the guilty person, his or her presence in the Republic of Latvia is not permissible.

(11) Expulsion from the Republic of Latvia is not applicable to a citizen of a European Union Member State, a European Economic Area state or the Swiss Confederation, as well as his or her spouse, children up to the age of twenty-one, parents or a person with whom he or she lives together and with whom he or she has a common (undivided) household or who

is in his or her care, except in cases where the court finds that, taking into account the circumstances of the case, the nature of the criminal offence and the personality of the guilty person, his or her presence in the Republic of Latvia poses a serious threat to public order or state security. If such a person has resided in the Republic of Latvia for the previous ten years or is a minor, then expulsion in respect of him or her shall be applied only if the court finds that, taking into account the circumstances of the case, the nature of the criminal offence and the personality of the guilty person, his or her presence in the Republic of Latvia poses a serious threat to state security. Expulsion from the Republic of Latvia in respect of a minor shall also be applied if it is in the interests of the minor in accordance with international agreements binding on the Republic of Latvia.

(2)	This penalty may be imposed as an additional penalty together with deprivation of liberty or a fine, imposing a prohibition on entry for a period of three to ten years and executing it after serving the principal penalty or after release from serving the penalty before the term in the manner prescribed by law, or after the entry into force of the judgment in the case of conditional conviction. The time of serving the additional penalty shall be counted from the day the person is expelled from the Republic of Latvia.
(With amendments made by the laws of 13.12.2007., 13.12.2012. and 16.10.2014., which enter into force on 01.02.2015.)

44. Section. Restriction of rights

(1)	Restriction of rights is the deprivation of certain rights or the imposition of a prohibition that does not allow a person to exercise certain rights, hold a certain office, engage in a certain professional or other type of activity, visit certain places or events.

(2)	Restriction of rights is an additional penalty imposed by a court or, when drawing up a penal order, determined by a prosecutor for a period of one year to five years, depriving the rights specified in the relevant section of the Special Part of this law or imposing a prohibition. Taking into account the type and nature of the criminal offence, in cases specifically provided for in the Special Part of this law, a person's rights may also be restricted for a longer period, but not longer than ten years.

(3)	According to the nature of the criminal offence, restriction of rights may also be imposed in cases where this penalty is not provided for in the sanction of the relevant section of the Special Part of this law, or in addition to the restriction of rights provided for in the sanction of the relevant section of the Special Part of this law, another restriction of rights may also be imposed.

(4)	If a person is convicted with deprivation of liberty and with restriction of rights, then the prohibition referred to in this section applies both to the time when the person is serving the deprivation of liberty, as well as to the period of serving the additional penalty specified in the judgment, counted from the day he or she finishes serving the principal penalty. When imposing this additional penalty together with conditional deprivation of liberty, the period of serving the additional penalty shall be counted from the day the specified probation period begins for the person. When imposing this additional penalty together with other types of principal penalty, the period of serving the additional penalty shall be counted from the day the conviction judgment or the prosecutor's penal order enters into force.
(In the wording of the law of 13.12.2012. with amendments made by the laws of 12.11.2015., 10.03.2016. and 05.10.2023., which enter into force on 20.10.2023.)

44.	1 Section. Prohibition to stand as a candidate in Saeima, European Parliament, republican city council and municipality council elections
(Deleted by the law of 13.12.2012., which enters into force on 01.04.2013.)

45. Section. Police supervision

(Deleted by the law of 08.07.2011., which enters into force on 01.10.2011. The amendment on the deletion of the section enters into force on 01.01.2015. See transitional provision paragraph 10)

45.	1 Section. Probation supervision
(Deleted by the law of 17.12.2020., which enters into force on 01.01.2022.)

V	Chapter Determination of penalty

46. Section. General principles of determination of penalty

(1)	The penalty shall be determined in the amount provided for the committed criminal offence by the sanction of the relevant section of the Special Part of this law, observing the provisions of the General Part of this law.

(2)	When determining the type of penalty, the nature of the committed criminal offence and the harm caused, as well as the personality of the guilty person, shall be taken into account.

(3)	When determining the measure of penalty, mitigating and aggravating circumstances of liability shall be taken into account.

(31) If the sanction of a section of the Special Part of this law for the committed crime provides for only one principal penalty — deprivation of liberty, when determining its duration, the nature of the criminal offence, the harm caused, the personality of the guilty person, as well as mitigating and aggravating circumstances of liability shall be taken into account.

(4)	The penalty of deprivation of liberty for a criminal violation and a less serious crime shall be applied if the purpose of the penalty cannot be achieved by imposing one of the lighter types of penalty provided for in the sanction of the relevant section.
(In the wording of the law of 13.12.2012. with amendments made by the law of 07.04.2022., which enters into force on 04.05.2022.)

47. Section. Mitigating circumstances of liability

(1)	The following circumstances shall be recognised as mitigating liability:

1)	the perpetrator of the criminal offence came forward as guilty, sincerely confessed and regretted what was done;

2)	the guilty person actively facilitated the detection and investigation of the criminal offence;

3)	the guilty person voluntarily compensated the victim for the harm caused by the criminal offence or eliminated the harm caused;

4)	the guilty person facilitated the detection of another person's crime;

5)	the criminal offence was committed under the influence of unlawful or immoral conduct of the victim;

6)	the criminal offence was committed in violation of the conditions of lawfulness of necessary defence, extreme necessity, apprehension of a person who has committed a criminal offence, justified professional risk, execution of an order and instruction;

7)	the criminal offence was committed by a person in a state of diminished capacity.

(2)	When determining the penalty, another circumstance related to the committed criminal offence, which is not provided for in this law, may also be recognised as a mitigating circumstance of liability.

(3)	A circumstance that is provided for in this law as an element of the composition of a criminal offence shall not be recognised as a mitigating circumstance of liability.
(With amendments made by the law of 13.12.2012., which enters into force on 01.04.2013.)

48. Section. Aggravating Circumstances

(1)	The following circumstances may be recognised as aggravating:

1)	the criminal offence constitutes recidivism of criminal offences;

2)	the criminal offence was committed by a group of persons;

3)	the criminal offence was committed by abusing an official position or the trust of another person;

4)	the criminal offence caused serious consequences;

5)	the criminal offence was committed against a woman, knowing that she is pregnant;

6)	the criminal offence was committed against a person who has not reached the age of eighteen, or against a person by exploiting their helplessness or infirmity due to age;

7)	the criminal offence was committed against a person by exploiting their official, material or other dependence on the guilty person;

8)	the criminal offence was committed with particular cruelty or by mocking the victim;

9)	the criminal offence was committed by exploiting circumstances of public disaster or during a state of emergency or state of exception;

10)	the criminal offence was committed using weapons or explosives or in another generally dangerous manner;

11)	the criminal offence was committed out of mercenary motives;

12)	the criminal offence was committed under the influence of alcohol, narcotic, psychotropic, toxic or other intoxicating substances;

13)	the person who committed the criminal offence, with the intention of obtaining a reduction of punishment, has provided knowingly false information about a criminal offence committed by another person;

14)	the criminal offence was committed out of racist, national, ethnic or religious motives or social hatred;

15)	a criminal offence involving violence or threat of violence, or a criminal offence against morality and sexual inviolability was committed against a person with whom the perpetrator of the criminal offence is related in the first or second degree, or against a spouse or former spouse, or against a person with whom the perpetrator of the criminal offence is or has been in a permanent intimate relationship, or against a person with whom the perpetrator of the criminal offence has a common (undivided) household;

16)	a criminal offence involving violence or threat of violence, or an intentional criminal offence against a person's health or morality and sexual inviolability was committed in the presence of a minor;

17)	the person who committed the criminal offence has given knowingly false testimony.

(2)	Taking into account the nature of the criminal offence, any of the circumstances referred to in paragraph one of this Section may not be recognised as aggravating.

(3)	When imposing a punishment, circumstances that are not specified in this law may not be recognised as aggravating.

(4)	A circumstance that is provided for in this law as an element of the composition of a criminal offence shall not be recognised as an aggravating circumstance.
(With amendments made by the laws of 27.05.2004., 12.10.2006., 21.10.2010., 13.12.2012., 15.05.2014., 25.09.2014.,
08.06.2017., 26.04.2018., 11.06.2020. and 06.07.2021., which entered into force on 05.08.2021.)

49. Section. Imposition of a Lighter Punishment than the Punishment Provided for by Law

(1)	If the court, taking into account several mitigating circumstances of the guilty person and the personality of the guilty person, considers it necessary to impose a punishment that is lower than the minimum limit provided for by law for the relevant criminal offence, it may mitigate the punishment accordingly, obligatorily stating the reasons for such a decision in the judgment.

(11)	When imposing a custodial sentence that is lower than the minimum limit provided for the relevant criminal offence in the Special Part of this law, a person may not be sentenced to less than:

1)	half of the minimum punishment provided for in the sanction of the relevant section for the commission of a serious crime;

2)	two-thirds of the minimum punishment provided for in the sanction of the relevant section for the commission of a particularly serious crime.

(2)	The court, taking into account several mitigating circumstances of the guilty person and the personality of the guilty person, for the commission of a serious crime for which this law provides for deprivation of liberty for a period longer than five years, and for the commission of a particularly serious crime, may impose a lighter type of punishment if the relevant crime has not caused the death of a person, has not caused serious bodily injury to at least one person or less serious bodily injury to several persons, is not related to violence or threat of violence, is not related to the illegal circulation of narcotic and psychotropic substances and was not committed by an organised group.

(3)	Paragraphs one and two of this Section shall not apply if the court has found that the criminal offence was committed in aggravating circumstances.

(4)	(Deleted by the law of 08.11.2007.)
(With amendments made by the laws of 25.04.2002., 08.11.2007., 13.12.2012. and 08.06.2017., which entered into force on 01.01.2018.)

49. 1 Section. Imposition of Punishment if the Right to Completion of Criminal Proceedings within a Reasonable Time has not been Observed

(1)	If the court finds that a person's right to completion of criminal proceedings within a reasonable time has not been observed, it may:

1)	take this circumstance into account when imposing punishment and mitigate the punishment;

2)	impose a punishment that is lower than the minimum limit provided for by law for the relevant criminal offence;

3)	impose another, lighter type of punishment than that provided for by law for the relevant criminal offence.

(2)	If the court finds that a person's right to completion of criminal proceedings within a reasonable time has not been observed and the person has committed a crime for which life imprisonment is provided for in the sanction of a section of the Special Part of the Criminal Law, the court may impose deprivation of liberty for twenty years instead of life imprisonment.
(In the wording of the law of 21.10.2010. with amendments made by the law of 01.12.2011., which entered into force on 01.01.2012.)

This excerpt sits under: Publisher: Saeima Type: law Adopted: 17.06.1998.

50. Section. Determination of Punishment for Multiple Criminal Offences

(1)	If a person has committed several independent criminal offences, the court, when rendering a judgment, or the prosecutor, when drawing up a penal order, shall determine the punishment separately for each criminal offence. In such case, the final punishment shall be determined according to the aggregate of criminal offences, by absorbing the lighter punishment into the more severe one or by fully or partially adding together the imposed punishments.

(2)	If all the criminal offences that constitute an aggregate of criminal offences are criminal violations or less serious crimes, the final punishment shall be determined by absorbing the lighter punishment into the more severe one or by fully or partially adding together the imposed punishments. In such case, the total amount or term of the punishment may exceed the maximum amount or term of the punishment provided for the most serious of the committed criminal offences, but not by more than half of the maximum amount or term of the punishment provided for the most serious of the committed criminal offences.

(3)	If at least one criminal offence that constitutes an aggregate of criminal offences is a serious or particularly serious crime, the final punishment shall be determined by fully or partially adding together the imposed punishments. In such case, the total amount or term of the punishment may exceed the maximum amount or term of the punishment provided for the most serious of the committed criminal offences, but not by more than half of the maximum amount or term of the punishment provided for the most serious of the committed criminal offences. The total term of added deprivation of liberty punishments (except life imprisonment) may not exceed twenty-five years, but if a particularly serious crime has been committed as a result of which the death of the victim has occurred, the total term of deprivation of liberty punishment may also be determined for life (life imprisonment).

(4)	An additional punishment, like the principal punishment, shall first be determined separately for each criminal offence, but then according to the aggregate of criminal offences together with the principal punishment. To the principal punishment determined according to the aggregate of criminal offences shall be added the additional punishments imposed separately for each criminal offence.

(41) If the principal punishment and the additional punishment correspond to one type of punishment, then they shall be fully or partially added together, determining the final punishment as the principal punishment. In such case, the total amount or term of the punishment may exceed the maximum amount or term provided for the respective type of principal punishment, but not by more than half thereof.

(5)	In the same manner the court shall determine the punishment if, after the entry into force of the judgment or the prosecutor's penal order, it is established that the person is guilty of another criminal offence which he or she committed before the entry into force of the judgment or the prosecutor's penal order in the first case. In this case, the punishment that has already been fully or partially served pursuant to the first judgment or the prosecutor's penal order shall be included in the term of the punishment. If the term of deprivation of liberty determined conditionally in the judgment is greater than the term of deprivation of liberty determined in another judgment, then the term of deprivation of liberty determined conditionally shall be fully or partially added together with the term of deprivation of liberty.

(6)	The total amount or term of the final punishment determined in accordance with the procedure of this Section may exceed the maximum amount or term determined for the respective type of punishment.
(With amendments made by the laws of 21.10.2010., 13.12.2012., 25.09.2014., 08.06.2017., 17.12.2020. and 07.04.2022.
which entered into force on 04.05.2022.)

51. Section. Determination of Punishment According to Multiple Judgments

(1)	If the convicted person, after the entry into force of the judgment but before the complete serving of the punishment, has committed a new criminal offence, the court in the new judgment shall fully or partially add to the punishment determined therein the punishment that has not been served pursuant to the previous judgment.

(11)	When determining the punishment according to multiple judgments, not less than one third of the unserved punishment shall be added to the punishment,
if:

1)	a person who has been punished for a serious or particularly serious crime has committed a serious or particularly serious crime during the unserved punishment term;

2)	recidivism of criminal offences has been established;

3)	the new criminal offence has been committed during the probationary period determined by the court;

4)	the new criminal offence has been committed during the time when the person has been conditionally released before the term from serving the principal punishment.

(2)	The final punishment according to multiple judgments must be greater than the punishment determined for the newly committed criminal offence, as well as the part of the unserved punishment pursuant to the previous judgment.

(3)	When adding together punishments according to multiple judgments, the total amount or term of the punishment may exceed the maximum amount or term determined for the respective type of punishment, but not by more than half of the maximum amount or term determined for the respective type of punishment. The total term of added deprivation of liberty punishments (except life imprisonment) may not exceed thirty years, but if a particularly serious crime has been committed as a result of which the death of the victim has occurred, the total term of deprivation of liberty punishment may also be determined for life (life imprisonment).

(31) If a person sentenced to life imprisonment who has been released from serving the punishment conditionally before the term commits a criminal offence during the pre-term supervision period, the court shall add life imprisonment to the determined punishment.

(4)	A judgment within the meaning of Sections 51 and 52 of this Law is also a prosecutor's penal order.
(With amendments made by the laws of 13.12.2007., 21.10.2010., 13.12.2012., 25.09.2014. and 11.06.2020. which
entered into force on 06.07.2020.)

52. Section. Rules for aggregation and substitution of punishments

(1)	When aggregating punishments for several criminal offences in one judgment or under several judgments, one day of deprivation of liberty corresponds to:

1)	(deleted by law of 13.12.2012.);

2)	eight hours of community service;

3)	(deleted by law of 08.07.2011.);

4)	two days of probation supervision.

(2)	A fine, restriction of rights, if these punishments are imposed together with deprivation of liberty, probation supervision or community service, shall be executed independently. In the case of conditional conviction, the additional punishment — community service — shall be executed independently, commencing with the probation period.

(21)	If for several criminal offences in one judgment or under several judgments principal punishments have been imposed — community service and deprivation of liberty, the term of which together does not exceed five years —, then the punishment

— community service — shall be executed independently. In such cases the execution of community service shall commence after serving the punishment of deprivation of liberty, but in the case of conditional conviction — immediately after the judgment enters into force.

(22)	If for several criminal offences in one judgment or under several judgments punishments have been imposed — community service and probation supervision —, then the community service shall be executed simultaneously with probation supervision.

(23)	If for several criminal offences in one judgment or under several judgments principal punishments have been imposed — probation supervision and deprivation of liberty, which has not been imposed conditionally and the term of which together does not exceed five years —, then the punishment — probation supervision — shall be executed independently. In such cases the execution of probation supervision shall commence after serving the punishment of deprivation of liberty.

(3)	When imposing punishments not mentioned in the first part of this Section, the court, taking into account detention, the part of the punishment served or the period of application of coercive measures of a medical nature, may mitigate the punishment or completely release the guilty person from serving the punishment.

(4)	The terms of punishment are counted in years, months and days. The court shall count detention in the term of punishment, counting one day of detention as one day of deprivation of liberty. The court shall count apprehension in the term of punishment, counting apprehension up to twenty-four hours as one day of deprivation of liberty. Detention that coincides in time with the serving of a punishment of deprivation of liberty for another criminal offence shall not be counted in the term of punishment.

(5)	Detention, apprehension and the part of the punishment served shall be counted in the term of the more severe punishment in accordance with the provisions of the first part of this Section.

(6)	House arrest shall be counted in the term of punishment of deprivation of liberty. One day of house arrest corresponds to one day of deprivation of liberty.

(7)	Substituted punishments shall be aggregated in full.
(With amendments made by laws of 12.06.2003., 28.09.2005., 16.06.2009., 08.07.2011., 13.12.2012., 15.05.2014.,
08.06.2017., 06.06.2019., 11.06.2020. and 17.12.2020., which enters into force on 01.01.2022.)

53. Section. Imposition of punishment for preparation for a crime and for an attempt of a crime

When imposing punishment for preparation for a crime or for an attempt of a crime, the court shall take into account the nature of the actions committed by the guilty person and the harm caused by them, the degree of realisation of the criminal intent and the reasons why the crime was not completed.

54. Section. Imposition of punishment for a criminal offence committed in complicity

(1)	When imposing punishment on participants in a criminal offence, the court shall take into account the nature and role of each participant's participation in the committed criminal offence.

(2)	Aggravating and mitigating circumstances relating to an individual participant shall be taken into account by the court when imposing punishment only on that participant.

55. Section. Conditional conviction

(1)	If, when imposing a punishment — deprivation of liberty — longer than three months but not longer than five years, or not longer than three years if a particularly serious crime has been committed, the court, taking into account the nature of the committed criminal offence and the harm caused, the personality of the guilty person and other circumstances of the case, obtains the conviction that the guilty person, without serving the punishment, will not commit offences in the future, it may convict the guilty person conditionally.

(2)	In this case the court shall decide not to execute the punishment if during the probation period determined by it the convicted person does not commit a new criminal offence, does not violate public order and fulfils the obligations provided for in the law regulating the execution of criminal punishments and established by the punishment execution institution.

(3)	When convicting conditionally, the court shall determine a probation period from six months to five years. The probation period shall commence on the day the court judgment enters into force. The determined probation period may not be less than the imposed term of deprivation of liberty.

(4)	When convicting conditionally, the judgment shall indicate the circumstances due to which the court has considered it appropriate not to execute the punishment.

(5)	When convicting conditionally, additional punishments may be imposed. The additional punishment — probation supervision — shall be executed only if the court decides to execute the principal punishment determined in the judgment.

(6)	(Deleted by law of 16.10.2014.)

(7)	(Deleted by law of 16.10.2014.)

(8)	(Deleted by law of 16.10.2014.)

(9)	If a conditionally convicted person without justifiable reason does not fulfil the obligations provided for in the law regulating the execution of criminal punishments or established by the punishment execution institution, the court, on the basis of a submission by the punishment execution institution, may adopt a decision on the execution of the punishment determined in the judgment for the convicted person or on the extension of the probation term up to one year.

(10)	If a conditionally convicted person commits a new criminal offence during the probation period, the punishment imposed on him or her shall be executed and the court shall impose punishment in accordance with the provisions provided for in Sections 51 and 52 of this law.

(101) If a conditionally convicted person has committed a new criminal offence through negligence and for the new criminal offence he or she has been released from criminal liability in accordance with Section 58 of this law or released from punishment in accordance with Section 59 of this law, or for the new criminal offence a fine or community service has been imposed on him or her as the principal punishment, the court may adopt a decision on the extension of the probation period up to one year.

(11)	Conditional conviction shall not be imposed on a person for the commission of an intentional crime if the person has previously been convicted with deprivation of liberty and the conviction for it has not been removed or expunged in the manner prescribed by law. Conditional conviction shall not be imposed on a person who has committed a crime provided for in Section 159 or 160 of this law.
(With amendments made by laws of 18.12.2003., 27.05.2004., 12.02.2004., 21.06.2007., 13.12.2007., 08.11.2007.,
08.07.2011., 13.12.2012., 25.09.2014., 16.10.2014., 08.06.2017., 11.06.2020. and 17.12.220., which enters into force on
01.01.2022.)

Chapter VI
Release from criminal liability and punishment

56. Section. Statute of Limitations for Criminal Liability

(1)	A person may not be brought to criminal liability if the following time has elapsed since the day on which they committed the criminal offence:

1)	(deleted by law of 21.10.2010.);

2)	two years from the day of commission of a criminal violation;

3)	five years from the day of commission of a less serious crime;

4)	ten years from the day of commission of a serious crime;

5)	fifteen years from the day of commission of a particularly serious crime, except for a crime for which life imprisonment may be imposed in accordance with law;

6)	(deleted by law of 12.11.2015.).

(11) A person may not be brought to criminal liability if twenty years have elapsed since the day on which the victim in a criminal offence directed against the morality and sexual inviolability of a minor or by which serious bodily injury associated with mutilation of the genital organs or loss of reproductive capacity has been caused, or in human trafficking, or in coercion to have an abortion, reached eighteen years of age, except for a crime for which life imprisonment may be imposed in accordance with law.

(2)	The limitation period shall be calculated from the day on which the criminal offence was committed, or in the case provided for in Paragraph 1.1 of this Section from the day on which the victim reached eighteen years of age, until the issuance of the indictment or the notification of an official extradition request to the accused, if the accused is staying in another country and a search for them has been declared.

(3)	The limitation period shall be interrupted if, before the expiry of the time limits specified in Paragraph one or 1.1 of this Section, the person who committed the criminal offence commits a new criminal offence. In this case, the limitation period provided for the most serious of the committed criminal offences shall begin to run from the moment of commission of the new criminal offence.

(4)	The question of the application of the statute of limitations to a person who has committed a crime for which life imprisonment may be imposed shall be decided by the court if thirty years have elapsed since the day of commission of the crime or since the day on which the victim in a crime directed against the morality and sexual inviolability of a minor reached eighteen years of age.
(With amendments made by laws of 20.05.2004., 28.09.2005., 12.10.2006., 21.10.2010., 01.12.2011., 13.12.2012.,
12.11.2015. and 08.06.2017., which enters into force on 01.01.2018.)

57. Section. Non-Application of Statute of Limitations

The statute of limitations for criminal liability shall not apply to a person who has committed a crime against humanity, a crime against peace, a war crime or has participated in genocide.

58. Section. Release from Criminal Liability

(1)	A person who has committed an insignificant criminal offence may be released from criminal liability.

(2)	A person who has committed a criminal violation or a less serious crime, except for criminal offences resulting in the death of a person, may be released from criminal liability if there is a reconciliation with the victim or their representative and the person has not been released from criminal liability in the last year for the commission of an intentional criminal offence by concluding a reconciliation, and has fully remedied the harm caused by the committed criminal offence or compensated for the damage caused.

(3)	An accused person who has substantially assisted in revealing a serious or particularly serious crime committed by another person, which is more serious or more dangerous than the criminal offence committed by them, may be released from criminal liability if this other person has been brought to criminal liability for this crime. This condition shall not apply to a person who is being brought to criminal liability for the commission of a particularly serious crime provided for in Sections 116, 117, 118, 125, 159,
160, 176, 190.1, 251, 252 and 253.1 of this Law, or to a person who themselves created or led an organised group or gang.

(4)	A person may also be released from criminal liability in cases specifically provided for in the Special Part of this Law.

(5)	A person may be released from criminal liability if it is established that their right to completion of criminal proceedings within a reasonable time has not been observed.

(6)	A person may be released from criminal liability if they committed a criminal offence at a time when they were subjected to human trafficking and were forced to commit it.
(With amendments made by laws of 25.04.2002., 28.09.2005., 21.05.2009., 21.10.2010., 13.12.2012., 11.06.2020. and
07.04.2022., which enters into force on 04.05.2022.)

58.	1 Section. Conditional Release from Criminal Liability

(1)	A person who has committed a criminal violation or a less serious crime and who has not been convicted of an intentional criminal offence, and against whom criminal proceedings have not been terminated in the last five years by conditional release from criminal liability, may be conditionally released from criminal liability by the prosecutor if, taking into account the nature of the offence and the harm caused, information characterising the accused and other circumstances of the case, a conviction is obtained that the accused will not commit criminal offences in the future.

(11) The prosecutor may also conditionally release from criminal liability, in the manner prescribed by law, a person who is accused of committing a serious crime, who has not been convicted of an intentional criminal offence, and against whom criminal proceedings have not been terminated in the last five years

by conditional release from criminal liability, and who has substantially assisted in revealing a serious or particularly serious crime committed by another person, which is more serious or more dangerous than the criminal offence committed by them, if this other person has been brought to criminal liability for this crime. This condition shall not apply to a person who is being brought to criminal liability for the commission of a serious crime provided for in Sections 125, 159, 160, 176, 190.1, 251, 252 and 253.1 of this Law, or to a person who themselves was the organiser of the crime.

(2)	When conditionally releasing from criminal liability, the prosecutor decides not to continue the criminal prosecution of the person for this offence if during the probationary period they do not commit a new criminal offence and fulfil the imposed obligations, and also, in the case where a reconciliation has been concluded, fulfil its conditions.

(3)	When conditionally releasing from criminal liability, the prosecutor shall set a probationary period for the person from three to eighteen months. The probationary period shall begin on the day on which the prosecutor's decision enters into force.

(4)	When conditionally releasing from criminal liability, the prosecutor may, with the person's consent, impose an obligation on them to:

1)	apologise to the victim;

2)	remedy the harm caused within a specified time limit;

21) (deleted by law of 07.04.2022.);

3)	appear periodically at the sentence enforcement institution and participate in probation programmes in accordance with the instructions of the sentence enforcement institution;

31) (deleted by law of 07.04.2022.);

4)	refrain from certain types of conduct or occupation;

5)	appear periodically at the sentence enforcement institution and visit a specialist designated by this institution to carry out addiction diagnostics, and comply with the specialist's instructions, including treatment for alcohol, narcotic, psychotropic, toxic substance or other addiction;

6)	pay a security deposit within a specified time limit.

(41) The amount of the security deposit shall be determined by the prosecutor, taking into account the nature of the criminal offence and the harm caused by it, the person's financial situation, as well as the type and measure of the penalty provided for by law.

(5)	If a person who has been conditionally released from criminal liability commits a new intentional criminal offence during the probationary period or fails to fulfil the imposed obligations or the conditions of reconciliation, their criminal prosecution shall be continued. In such cases, the paid security deposit shall be credited to the state budget.
(In the wording of law of 20.06.2002. with amendments made by laws of 18.12.2003., 27.05.2004., 21.06.2007., 21.05.2009.,
16.06.2009., 15.05.2014., 17.12.2020. and 07.04.2022., which enters into force on 04.05.2022. Point 6 of Paragraph four,
Paragraph 4.1 and the amendment regarding the supplementation of Paragraph five with a sentence enter into force on 03.11.2022. See Transitional Provisions of law of 07.04.2022.)

59. Section. Release from Punishment or Serving of Punishment
